## Historical 22 November 2026 — IDO/asm-processor padding blocker resolved; see the canonical six-function result above

- Reworked previously native-exact but ELF-blocked `func_8000AB30_B730` (0xB730; 68 bytes), together with genuinely unaccepted `func_8000AAE8_B6E8` (0xB6E8; 40 bytes) and `func_8000A18C_AD8C` (0xAD8C; 48 bytes): all **156/156 original MIPS instruction bytes exact** with original IDO5.3. No counted duplicate; rejected earlier seven accepted rediscoveries.
- Root-cause confirmed by native assembler experiments: original `func_8000AFA8_BBA8` ends after 752 bytes at `0xBE98`, followed by **two unowned NOPs** through `0xBEA0`. The vendored asm-processor counts NOPs inside one `GLOBAL_ASM` block as part of the ELF `FUNC` (760 vs 752), even though standalone GNU as honours 752 and a trailing `.size` is ineffective after asmprocessor merging. **New successful structural solution:** retain original segment split at `0xBEA0`; after Splat regeneration, fail-closed remove exactly the two post-`endlabel` NOP instructions from the embedded pragma fragment so the original linker supplies eight unowned alignment bytes. No instruction edits, extra function symbols, symbol-table patches, or changed ROM bytes.
- **Fresh clean pinned-upstream replay PASS:** complete original USA expanded 33,554,432 / 33,554,432 ROM bytes identical (SHA-1 `6ea0ed71032ce08fc2745f412d84936382197494`); all **7,376/7,376** positive-size ELF function identity tuples, including corrected 752B neighbor, and all **82,216/82,216** original-upstream ELF relocation tuples exact. Original SGI IDO5.3 `-O2 -mips2`. Candidate ELF SHA-256 `af291875532b674fde3070a460d356c611108af58c3088114eb4fdb1904d9a73`; sanitized report and fail-closed source verifier in `decomp/research/usa_unique_three_20261122/`.
- Host **GCC14.2 and Clang17 ASan/UBSan PASS**, 4,096 cases for each of three original sources; three synthetic padding/negative-control unittest methods PASS. Source-only downloadable ZIP delivered in session: `goemon_usa_padding_boundary_20261122.zip`, SHA-256 `6c0a448e8fb2b3c68ea92d1e0646fe834389126d946911fc1ec75c491e716d2b`. Record reproducible compiler behavior (including failed `.align`, anonymous ASM, grouping and source split attempts) in updated `decomp/COMPILER_QUIRKS.md` **in the same commit**.
- **NOT PROMOTED / NOT canonical G2:** full modern 1,965 accepted-USA stage `decomp.verify` (82,103 semantic relocations), strict inventory and full `decomp.local_ci` **NOT RUN**. Current GitHub tree cannot be cloned in native build container (GitHub DNS/TCP unavailable); original pinned upstream proof does not imply canonical integration. USA remains **1,965 accepted C / 84,648 original bytes**, original ASM preserved. Next action: materialize complete current-branch repository, integrate three using the verified padding treatment, run strict canonical gates before any promotion.

### 2026-11-14 — Thread-safe fast freeze engine at 100 Hz

- **Changed:** added a 10 ms independent background worker for active freeze locks, instead of relying on Tk's slower callback scheduling. Worker reads/writes game RAM only, never touches Tk. It sleeps longer when idle.
- **Efficiency:** compares each locked range first and skips matching values before additional gameplay checks/verified write. Maintains state checks immediately before changed writes, overlap limits, fixed big-endian bytes, readback and failure-stop semantics.
- **Concurrency:** added an RLock around freeze changes, polling and snapshots, with explicit APIs for removal and label changes. Connection swaps must acquire the same guard.
- **Limitation:** rapid or every-frame game overwrites can still become visible between checks; a truly frame-atomic lock requires modification of the game/runtime, not an out-of-process poller.
- **Tests:** added background-correction, stop/remove, session token and interval regression tests; these were authored but not executed against a live game in this connector environment.

### 2026-11-14 — Attach to existing Linux game PID with explicit OS permission support

- **Changed:** the Connection panel supports direct, explicitly selected PID attachment independently of the game launcher. Added a Linux-only permission control that, with explicit warning and polkit authentication, applies a temporary `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope=0` system setting, plus a control restoring the previous scope recorded in the toolkit session. Permission changes run in a worker; the Tk interface remains responsive.
- **Why:** Linux Yama ptrace scope often blocks same-user `process_vm_readv` against an independently launched game, even with a correct PID. PID selection alone cannot override kernel access rules. No permission changes happen automatically when connecting.
- **Guards:** only stock `pkexec` and `sysctl` binaries are executed for the requested setting; no game or toolkit executable runs as root. System-wide same-user ptrace isolation is relaxed only after the user accepts the warning. The toolkit never writes permanent sysctl configuration, and process ownership / Flatpak limits still apply.
- **Tests:** added OS helper unit tests for scope reporting, the authenticated command and invalid value blocking. Live polkit/game tests not executed in this connector environment.
- **Next:** test external PID on Ubuntu, verify game memory scanning and restore original scope after use.

### 2026-11-14 — Strong 16-bit Mini Ebisumaru countdown evidence

- **Observed:** user follow-up video shows `0x8020C8E6/E7` = `00/00` normal and `FF/FF` miniature; `u16` reads 0 or 65535 (`s16` 0 or −1). `0x801C159A` reads 52 normal / 28 mini but can switch before the two-byte field; the upper half of `u32 0x8020C8E4` varies independently.
- **Manual-write observation:** user set just E6 to 255 from a normal state; E6 became 254 while E7 became 255 and counted down with borrow. This is precisely the big-endian `0xFF00 → 0xFEFF → 0xFEFE ...` decrement sequence. User reports transformation only works at all-0 or all-255 endpoints.
- **Changed:** added controlled-write evidence, sample timeline, arithmetic, confidence levels and original MIPS follow-up to `docs/EBISUMARU_TRANSFORMATION_RUNTIME_FINDINGS_2026-11-14.md`. Documentation-only change; no toolkit writes or controls added.

- **Limits:** countdown cadence, specific MIPS width, update ownership, and interpretation of downstream `0x801C159A` remain unknown. Do not extrapolate timer duration.
- **Next:** trace read/write/guard paths for the E6 halfword and identify `0x801C159A`'s role.

### 2026-11-14 — G64-087 state dispatcher (RESEARCH_ONLY)

- **Changed:** Added a ROM-backed trace of `func_8001F8B0_204B0` and a sanitized host reference for its three valid callback choices and invalid-selector address boundary. Kept the dispatcher `GLOBAL_ASM` after two bounded native candidates failed.
- **Why:** The dispatcher selects one of three callback pointers using `SYS_W+0x3ADDE`. The original has no range check or default path, so out-of-range selectors must not be turned into a safe behavior claim.

### 2026-11-13 — G64-045 VM setup callback ABI (RESEARCH_ONLY)

- **Changed:** recorded the observed owner offsets in an F37C-local research layout and updated its host harness for allocator halfword arguments, exact float bits, callback order, slot wrap and untouched bytes. Marked G64-045 `RESEARCH_ONLY`, added its execution report, and advanced the handoff to G64-046. Shared `script_vm.h`, production `script_vm.c`, manifest ownership, stored proof and inventory remain unchanged.
- **Why:** establish the field widths and allocator ABI from pinned instructions, then test one falsifiable float-staging/register-order hypothesis in the actual `script_vm.c` unit before spending further compiler variants.
- **Evidence:** F37C is 192 bytes at VMA `0x8003F37C` / ROM `0x3FF7C` with slice SHA-256 `74867178f34d8d45b1753520e1218d6e6c2307f44937258e95c3828f1cf1efd0`. The pinned allocator reads six stack arguments with `lhu`; F37C reads a word at owner `+0x8C`, reads/writes a byte at `+0xD8`, publishes the returned pointer, then registers two callbacks in order. The full baseline `script_vm.c` unit matched all 10,112 original bytes at VMA `0x8003CFA0`. The one corrected candidate was also 10,112 bytes, but differed in 36 words (115 bytes), all inside F37C. Its first mismatch is `+0x08` / `0x8003F384`: candidate `mtc1 $zero,$f2`, original scale `lwc1 $f2`; the scale LO16 moved from `+0x08` to `+0x0C`, and the allocator call moved from `+0x60` to `+0x5C`. Candidate/source/tool hashes and full details are in `decomp/plans/execution/2026-11-13-g64-045-vm-setup-abi.json`.
- **Tests:** the F37C ASan/UBSan host harness passed 300/300 synthetic cases, including positive/negative zero, infinities, NaN payloads, random bit patterns, slot wrap, callback order and untouched object/control bytes. A positive-zero-to-scale mutant failed at the expected float-bit assertion. The existing broader VM host harness passed under ASan/UBSan. An initial local-CI run caught a stale `script_vm.h` source fingerprint after a temporary shared-header edit; that edit was reverted and the layout remains candidate-local. The final `python3 -m decomp.local_ci` run passed: 162 decomp Python tests, 92 verifier/ledger tests, task/evidence validation, and the display-emitter ASan/UBSan fixture.
- **Limitations/next:** native G1 failed and no second trial was warranted because the predicted register-order discriminator failed and no measured first-mismatch improvement was available. Full-ROM G2, inventory and in-game verification were not run. Stored proof remains 153 matching-C functions / 11,800 bytes and 7,376 inventory entries. Continue with G64-046; keep F37C `GLOBAL_ASM`.

## 2026-11-13 — G64-082 math helper 8000E39C (PASSED_G2)

- **Reason and branch:** resumed the active 100-task run in the same worktree, `codex/decomp-100-task-plan`. The trace covered 43 direct call sites from 38 callers. Caller `0x801D9A00` passes scale in `$f12`, X in `$f14`, Y/Z float bits in `$a2/$a3`, and object via caller stack `+0x10`; nested position is object `+0x18`, coordinates are `+0x08/+0x0C/+0x10`, and results are at object `+0x70/+0x74/+0x78`.
- **Behavior:** computes `dx/dy/dz`, squares, `(dx² + dy²) + dz²`, and `scale²`; calls `_nsqrtf` on each `(delta² * scale²) / length²`, negates only on strict delta `< 0`, then returns 1. Negative scale is squared; signed-zero and NaN behavior follow the observed comparisons and FPU path.

V1 was 328 bytes with a `0x50` frame and first mismatch `+0x00`; v2 improved to `+0x24` but used incorrect position offsets; v3 used the original `0x48` frame but nine spill homes differed starting `+0x58`; v4 linked identically to v3; v5 moved the `scale_squared` definition after `dz_squared` and exactly matched all 328 bytes. IDO SHA-256 `76d796c9591c9f5504949f85b29c32a6039a663791afe2f36f34ca18425b64d0`; GNU MIPS ld SHA-256 `665c22b6b7c6543a9cdd9373c0e6f6a575db3ece870b133c6dae367706e3848b`.

Fresh G2 matched all 33,554,432 ROM bytes (SHA-1 `6ea0ed71032ce08fc2745f412d84936382197494`), all 150/150 claims and 11,476 bytes. Inventory passed: 163 C, 7,213 ASM, 7,376 entries, unchanged entry identity. `combined.json` SHA-256 `328be129fe1c39aa31e41a7b37472741a7b0ac968d67a3f17f7489e86ff4ed14`; `inventory.json` SHA-256 `16497366ac28e7aa5cb9d905985ae980acb09d97c97ed832f414bf6a31c6c943`. Five G64-091 input/attempt signatures were imported from preserved sources and ELFs and passed the existing ledger validator; the recorded queue runs explicitly state that no compiler was executed during import.

## 2026-11-13 — G64-081 math helper 8000E2D4 (RESEARCH_ONLY)

- **Reason and branch:** continued the v2 math wave in the same worktree, branch `codex/decomp-100-task-plan`, from clean commit `63685e4fb24a4cb055f43bb1fc899a6025edac13`; after fetching `origin/chatgptweb/decomp`, the remote matched. G64-002 remains `PASSED_G2`.

Its single direct caller at `0x80204CFC` passes scale 8.0, unsigned angles at `+0x14/+0x16`, and `$s1` as output; return value is ignored.
- **Behavior and tests:** angle A sine times magnitude writes `+0x74`; angle A cosine times magnitude forms the intermediate, then angle B sine/cosine multiply that intermediate into `+0x70/+0x78`. The helper functions mask to 10 bits; the target forwards the raw halfwords. Seven test-only reference calls passed ASan/UBSan across zero/quadrant axes, negative scale, signed zero, wrapped angles and exact binary32 result bits. A reassociated-multiplication mutant failed the expected `+0x70` bits.
- **Native trials:** three correctly typed pinned IDO 5.3 target-only candidates linked at `0x8000E2D4`. Best was 180/200 bytes and first differed at `+0x08`: candidate saves `$ra` at `sp+0x1C`, original stores `$a1` at `sp+0x24`. It also saves the live float in `$f20`. The first helper links the message task into owner-managed lists; the handler saves `$ra` and temporarily stores owner in the caller's argument-home word, with no saved `$s` registers or interrupt masking.

- Inspected `func_8003DBEC_3E7EC` original stack layout and compiled the exact alias candidate with pinned IDO 5.3. Original frame is **0x48**; candidate reserves **0x50**, but saves the same `$s0–$s7`, `$fp`/`$s8`, and `$ra` at `sp+0x18..0x3C` and uses `sp+0x44` for the outer count. No candidate stack access touches `sp+0x48..0x4F`. The two exact frame-only mismatches are entry `0x8003DBEC` (`-0x48` vs `-0x50`) and return delay `0x8003DDC0` (`+0x48` vs `+0x50`).
- Ran five controlled IDO source variants (inline script argument, post-callback state reload before cursor store, outer-counter `register` hint, terminal-state range check, and non-volatile work pointer). All still emitted a **0x50** frame; sizes were **456–476 bytes**, and no full branch layout matched. All six sources (including baseline) passed **5,000** original-MIPS/host-C differential cases each over seeds `0x1D8` and `0xDEADBEEF` (**30,000 total**). Trial sources, objects and detailed diagnostics remain in the ignored `practice/diagnostics/decomp-100-inputs/g64-032/` directory.
- The baseline emits all six branch-likely instructions, but four PCs are displaced: original `DC8C/DCC0/DCD0/DD14/DD50/DD8C`; candidate `DC90/DCC4/DCD8/DD14/DD58/DD8C`. The direct probe reports **53/95** non-relocation words when it excludes 23 candidate relocation sites; the separate relocation-layout report records **53/88** comparable words plus **17** relocation-layout differences. These are distinct comparison rules. The alias candidate comment now also states the 0x50/0x48 frame mismatch.
- No production C, manifest, proof, or function ownership changed. The target remains `GLOBAL_ASM`; no original-address link, new full-ROM/inventory run, or in-game test was performed. Evidence: [`decomp/VM_TEXT_LOOP_FRAME.md`](decomp/VM_TEXT_LOOP_FRAME.md) and `decomp/plans/execution/2026-11-11-g64-032-text-loop-frame.json`.

## 2026-11-09 - VM text-loop aliasing/control-flow research: 47→53 comparable instructions (UNPROMOTED)

Most failed to improve IDO scheduling. The useful change was **one evidence-bound script-word load/store aliasing assumption**: treat the VM work pointer as volatile, explicitly load its script word into `script_arg` just before the step callback, and store the result via the work pointer. This coaxes IDO to load `$a1` from saved `$s1` before `jal` and fill the retry branch delay slot with the correct reload. The isolated function remains **472 bytes**, improves to **53/95 exact comparable instruction words** with 23 relocation positions, and still has the incorrect `0x50` stack frame; it is **not** an exact match.

- Executed a local original-MIPS-vs-host-C differential interpreter over **10,000 synthetic scenarios** (5,000 each with deterministic seeds `0x1d8`, `0xbeef`), using the original instructions from the hash-verified local ROM. ## Current status — 2026-11-01

**Baseline:** v0.9.9 source recovered from the original release ZIP. The deleted conversation was reconstructed separately from 102 screenshots; screenshots and personal conversation transcript are not committed to this public repository.

**Verified locally (source audit, 2026-11-01):**
- Python `pytest -q`: **84 passed, 5 subtests passed**.
- Python compilation: `python -m compileall -q autosplitter practice tests` passed.
- Portable C checkpoint/watch unit tests compiled with `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and passed in a simulated environment.
- **Not verified:** launching the Windows GUI and observing scene/character operations on a running US Goemon64Recomp instance. Automated tests are not evidence of successful in-game warps.

**Working implementation (based on source inspection):**
- Windows-targeted game-memory connection and practice UI.
- LiveSplit autosplitter bridge/engine and an import path for route definitions.
- Read-only scene/transition tracing and native debug-menu observation tools.
- Guarded live N64 guest-memory editing; dangerous fields require explicit additional opt-in.
- Logical checkpoint/character state assistance (not a full N64 savestate).
- Portable C checkpoint/watch prototypes; **not yet installed in a custom N64 ROM**.

**Current blocker:** direct one-click scene warp. The experimental v0.9.7 pending-destination + exit-request write returned to the title screen. v0.9.9 correctly disables this unsafe route. Locate the actual engine scene/door loading routine; do not re-enable direct warp based only on map-ID changes.

**Observed normal door sequence (MACHI 353 → 351):**
- `0x800C7CA0` pending destination changed at ~2715 ms.
- `0x800C7AB2` current map changed at ~3112 ms (~397 ms later).
- `0x800C7ABC` previous map and `0x800C7AB4` stage offset changed with the committed current map.
- This establishes an observed order of values, **not** the invocation or address of a safe scene loader.

**Useful guest-RAM addresses (US build / experimental):**

| Address | Interpretation |
|---|---|
| `0x800C7A94` | Top-level game state; observed debug menu 10 and gameplay 13 |
| `0x800C7AA4` | Debug-menu category / context-dependent gameplay submode |
| `0x800C7AB2` | Current absolute stage/map ID |
| `0x800C7AB4` | Native stage offset |
| `0x800C7ABC` | Previous map ID |
| `0x800C7CA0` | Pending destination |
| `0x800C7A9F` | Suspected exit request; earlier direct write returned to title |
| `0x800C7E60` | Menu-entry pointer, **not** a standalone character ID |
| `0x8015C5DC` | Active gameplay character |

Known debug-menu category bases: SIRO 0, DOUCHU 300, MACHI 350. Additional mappings require validation. These addresses are observational findings, **not** authorisation to perform raw writes.

## Next development steps

1. Trace the native scene loader or door-transition function in the recompilation/reverse-engineered game code; corroborate against a real door transition.
2. Establish a minimal and safe state-preparation + load invocation, first in disposable saves with checks/rollback strategy.
3. Validate the change with a reproducible in-game test, capturing expected versus observed room, player state and failures.
4. Continue work on native N64 ROM menu integration only after the appropriate runtime and build integration are understood.
5. Keep the autosplitter independent from experimental writes and protect competitive-run integrity.

## 2026-11-02 — v0.11.3: Ushiwaka story-event probes, Sasuke battery UI, native pre-load character test

- **Ushiwaka first A/B JSON** `state_probe_20261102_110106_887579.json`: Goemon (character 0), map 371, 11:00:51.517→11:01:04.456 UTC, **before starting minigame vs after starting**. Within the persistent save event flag block, guest `0x8015C60D` changed **`0x20 → 0x30`**, bit **`0x10` set**. Strong *minigame-start/storyline-state* candidate. Other differences are transient/dynamic; no C captured. Needs controlled bit test before marking confirmed.
- **Ushiwaka second A/B JSON** `state_probe_20261102_110210_650667.json`: same character/map, 11:02:05.125→11:02:09.012 UTC, **before vs after blue-fish completion**. Same guest `0x8015C60D` changed **`0x30 → 0x38`**, bit **`0x08` set**. Blue fish quantity `0x8015C723` changed **`0x03 → 0x00`**. Bit 0x08 is a strong blue-fish completion candidate, not yet a confirmed Achilles' Heel grant or final quest completion. No C capture. Item ownership and storyline completion are separately checked in gameplay, per user's manual tests.
- **Sasuke second battery UI:** confirmed observational timing is first-battery bit `0x20`, then post-cutscene `0x40`: `0x8015C609` becomes `0x60` **at the end of** battery 2 cutscene. It is not the cutscene command. Added a clearly visible **read-only** indicator near the top of Unlocks (also already in Live Memory), plus both Ushiwaka candidate flags with explicit unverified labels.
- **Native cursor correction:** user's `0x800C7E60` persists at **128** once native menu has been opened. Original Goemon/Ebisumaru/Sasuke/Yae `0x8007BAD0...BAF4` pointer progression applied only while highlighting entries in the active native menu. The pointer is **not** a robust gameplay character selector and is never written to trigger a warp.
- **No C-Down workaround:** removed experimental scene browser's automatic post-load C-Down presses. Scene reload now optionally stages *only* global character `0x8015C5DC` (u32 0..3) **before** staging top-level game state `0x0C`, allowing the original initial actor/model loader to choose the character if it honours the ID. Source RE names spawn loader `func_801CB5D0` (reads this global) and `func_801CBC40` (initial model load); it also documents area-transition reset writes, so this is an **unverified hypothesis** until observed in-game. Does not modify per-task ID, menu pointer or XYZ. If pre-load staging fails, restores pending-map/character only while safely in source gameplay; after reload it only verifies both loaded global ID and task-local character ID. No automatic fallback or post-load writes on failure. Party-unlocked status not required for the *experimental initial loader*; this is different from normal C-Down party swapping.
- **Tests:** `xvfb-run -a python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` **171 passed** in simulated game RAM / Linux Tk. Covers pre-load write, invalid-character no-write, character-write rollback, no post-load C-Down, wrong-char result and new Unlocks displays. No Windows in-game validation; potential actor mismatch, wrong spawn, crash/softlock. Use disposable saves.
- **Next:** Observe whether the actor/model actually spawns as chosen in US Goemon64Recomp. If it does not, trace `func_80021444` transition reset and the native menu's character-to-spawn commit instead of post-loading swaps.

## 2026-11-02 — Character-aware experimental reload and battery cutscene timing
- **Live user observation:** second Sasuke battery completion changes packed event byte `0x8015C609` from `0x20` to `0x60` **after** the pickup cutscene ends. Thus bit `0x40` is an observed second-cutscene-complete marker, not evidence of the cutscene trigger. Added a read-only decoded Live Memory watch; did not add an unverified event-trigger write.
- **Character scan:** user-repeated native debug cursor sweep (Goemon/Ebisumaru/Sasuke/Yae then reverse) in map 404, menu state 10, 8 stable samples each. `0x800C7E60` four-byte pointer follows `0x8007BAD0 + 0x0C*character` (0/1/2/3). `0x800C7E62`/`63` are overlapping truncated representations, not independent fields. This is the menu-entry pointer, **not an integer character ID** and should not be written outside the original menu. Other 60 hits are lookup patterns without standalone semantic proof.
- **Fix:** Scene browser's character dropdown was completely ignored by experimental 0x0C reload. It is now captured with the destination and, after 3 playable samples, applied through the original game C-Down switch if that character is already unlocked in the party. Verify idle action, map, global character and per-task ID after native model swap. Up to 4 spaced native key presses or manual C-Down fallback; timeout, wrong map and missing party character fail explicitly. Never directly write character ID, task, or menu-pointer addresses. Native menu may load even locked characters; this fallback cannot mimic that.

## 2026-11-02 — v0.11.0 cross-area logical checkpoint restore and responsive connection

- **Cross-area checkpoints:** Restore now detects if the live map differs from the checkpoint map. It requests the user-tested experimental stage reload by writing the saved map to `0x800C7CA0` and setting game state `0x800C7A94=0x0C`. It **does not** write saved XYZ, rotation, save data, resources or headers during the reload. A polled state machine waits until the actual loaded map ID equals the checkpoint map, ordinary gameplay (`0x0D`), no transitions/scripts, an idle live player, and at least three stable player-pointer observations after a minimum settling delay; only then does it call the existing guarded logical restore, which writes the checkpoint's exact saved coordinates. It preserves the existing native C-Down character-switch path if the loaded character differs. Same-area restores remain immediate. Cancel, a wrong loaded map, timeout and terminal write errors prevent unsafe follow-on writes; final restore errors are not retried.
- **Responsive Connect:** RAM discovery, process opening and snapshot validation moved to a daemon worker thread. Tk is updated only on its main thread via a polled completion queue, and Connect is disabled during discovery. The UI remains responsive even if scanning takes time, but discovery itself may still be slow.
- **Tests:** `xvfb-run -a python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` with synthetic RDRAM and Tk UI, **161 passing tests** (plus nine discovery-class fixtures from imports in isolated targeted runs). Regression tests for cross-area load delays, script lock, timeout/wrong map, character swapping, terminal failed writes, same-area behaviour and responsiveness while a worker blocks.
- **Limits:** This is an experimental **logical** checkpoint, **not** an emulator savestate. Camera, actor spawns, enemies, animation/physics and active cutscenes cannot be reconstructed. The game may move/snap coordinates after the tool writes them, or its forced reload may softlock. No real Windows game integration run has been performed. A disposable/backed-up save is required for safe experimentation.

## 2026-11-02 — weapon index confirmed; map, magic and player-task false positives refined

- **Equipped weapon:** `0x8015C607` (one byte) is user-confirmed across Goemon, Ebisumaru, Sasuke and Yae. Normal values `0x01`, `0x02`, `0x03` select the three current-character weapons. Manually writing out-of-range values accesses unintended next-character weapon artwork/behaviour: Goemon -> Ebisumaru hammer; Ebisumaru -> Sasuke kunai; Sasuke -> Yae sword; Yae -> what user described as `letter 1`. Exact out-of-bounds dispatch mechanics not proven. Added built-in live watch, showing unusual values as CHECK, and unsafe-edit policy permitting any single byte.
- **Map overlay:** `0x8015C574` *reads* `00` while hidden and `01` while displayed (new live user evidence). However manual writes of any tested value open the map, including `00` which does not close it. Separate read behaviour from write behaviour; never implement an `00=close` button from this evidence. Added observed-state live watch.
- **Rejected magic candidate:** `0x800C7A73` oscillates `00/01` outside magic activation. It is NOT a confirmed magic-active flag; find another source through activation-specific testing.
- **Rejected weapon candidate:** `0x8014C340` oscillates `0x40`/`0x41` and sometimes `0x00` independently. Not a dedicated selected-weapon field.
- **Velocity reassessment:** dynamic task float fields `+0x68`/`+0x70`/`+0x74`/`+0x78`/`+0x7C` (observed at `0x8016A0F8`/`0x8016A100`/`0x8016A104`/`0x8016A108`/`0x8016A10C` for one session) read zero while stationary but fluctuate during NPC conversations, weakening their identification as direct locomotion velocity. User reports maximum byte-readings ~192 (and ~191 on `+0x7C`); these are likely first bytes of IEEE-754 floats, e.g. `0xC0=192` is part of `-2.7127` at `+0x68`, **not a 192-unit velocity maximum**. Watch entire four-byte big-endian f32, and always resolve the task pointer dynamically.
- Automated tests now verify built-in equipped-weapon watch, map observed-state watch and unsafe out-of-range weapon edits. Windows gameplay not reproduced inside test runner.

- Added four documented **Miracle Items** at `0x8015C85B`, `0x8015C85F`, `0x8015C863`, `0x8015C866`. Exact item/symbol correspondence (star, moon, etc.) is unknown. The last byte is the published `0xC866` address and needs verification on the running US build.
- The Live Memory **UNSAFE** checkbox now enables unrestricted 1/2/4/8-byte canonical guest hex values, including bypassing ordinary mapped field value limits and editing dynamic/watch addresses. Added a raw address/width/value editor for N64 guest RDRAM `0x80000000..0x807FFFFF`; compares original bytes and verifies readback. No Windows host address writes. This does **not** implement scene loading.
- Sprites tab now opens the exact Spriters Resource asset reference supplied by the user; original art remains optional, user-imported PNGs and is not committed in this public repository.
- Tests: see current test command output; Python mock memory and virtual display tests cannot establish in-game semantics or Windows runtime behavior.

### 2026-11-02 — Project64 connection and fragmented RDRAM discovery

- Changed: Dashboard connection selector for auto/recomp/project64; process selection rejects ambiguous instances instead of choosing the first PID. RAM discovery coalesces adjacent readable memory regions before scanning, preserving gaps and the existing pointer/player/endian validation. Corrected stale GUI version/host labels. Existing Python modules, autosplitter logic and portable C core are retained.
- Why: Project64's live 8 MiB RDRAM buffer was split into regions with different page protections, including small protected-code regions skipped by the previous minimum-region-size filter. The original scanner rejected all candidates despite valid original US addresses.
- Tested: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` — 172 passed on Windows, including Tk tests, fragmented-page scans, a signature across chunk boundaries, inaccessible-gap preservation, process ambiguity and read-only GUI worker selection. `git diff --check` passed. Read-only process scan and actual Tk Live memory connection to Project64 3.0.1.5664 succeeded: system pointer, Goemon ID, player/task pointers, map, HP/resources and coordinates decoded at existing guest addresses. User moved Goemon; successive probes changed map 561/XYZ (90, 0, -80) to map 353/XYZ approximately (-143.67, 0, -122.67). GUI showed map 353/Oedo Town South, Goemon, HP 10, Ryo 100 and lives 3. Test process handle was opened without write permissions. No game memory was written by these validation steps.
- Limitations: This verifies the sampled fields on one live US configuration, not every address, ROM revision, quest bit or cross-host write behavior. Project64 warps, checkpoint restores and writes remain untested. Discovery still requires an 8 MiB readable range and a loaded player; 4 MiB support is deferred. Portable C was unchanged and not rebuilt for this connection-only change. Existing executables require rebuilding/restarting to include it.
- Next: Use read-only State discovery on Project64 to collect repeated labelled event captures and compare candidates with existing recomp research; add an evidence registry and repeated-trial filtering before promoting any new flag semantics.

## Mandatory change-log template

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD — Short change title
- Changed: ...
- Why: ...
- Tested: exact commands/results; specify if in-game testing was NOT performed
- Remaining: ...
- Next: ...
```

**Commit rule:** Every functional/documentation change must update this file in the same commit. Push the commit to `main` (or push a feature branch and merge it) as part of completing the change; never claim a push succeeded without checking GitHub.

### 2026-11-21 — Guard draft task registry against malformed JSON value types

- **Changed:** On draft PR #154, make `decomp.task_registry.validate` report schema errors for non-object roots/tasks and non-string/unhashable SHA, ID, enum and conflict values. Add ROM-free malformed-input regression cases and verify the CLI returns an error instead of a Python traceback.
- **Why:** A malformed hand-edited registry could previously raise `TypeError` or `AttributeError` during validation rather than fail closed, even though the registry is meant to be a safe resumable task entry point.
- **Tested:** Reviewed the exact remote source and added deterministic unittest coverage. **Tests not executed in this session**: the full authenticated GitHub source checkout is unavailable through the local shell (GitHub DNS resolution fails); the draft remains pending full `python3 -m decomp.local_ci` on a complete checkout. No original-ROM/native G2/gameplay testing claimed.
- **Remaining:** Review and run full ROM-free CI with the combined current checkout before merging #154. Native C ownership and accepted manifests are unchanged.
- **Next:** Rebase #154 against merged #149/#150/#153 as needed and repeat the full local CI gate.
